=== PAGE 729 ===
[827]
[827]
Diagnostics Dashboard Overview - Part 1
Diagnostics Dashboard Overview - Part 1
The Diagnostics
Diagnostics Dashboard
Dashboard is a central location where we can review an overview of the operational health of our environment. We
have a number of different cards to go through, so lets get started!
1. Click Diagnostics
Diagnostics to open the Diagnostics Dashboard.
2.Overall Findings
Overall Findings -- Diagnostics Findings are derived from vCenter, ESXi, and vSAN events, properties, and product log data
which help identify potential problems or informs us about existing issues in our environment. This tile gives us a quick glance
into the total number of findings in our environment and a breakdown of how those findings are rated: Critical, Immediate, or
Warning.
3.Security Advisories
Security Advisories -- Of the number of Overall Diagnostic Findings, this tile shows us how many of those findings are
specifically related to Security Advisories (VMSAs, or VMware Security Advisories that are missing and need to be applied in
the environment). We also see the ratings of these missing Security Advisories: Critical, Immediate, or Warning.
4.Certificates
Certificates -- VMware Aria Operations provides centralized certificate management to monitor and manage all certificates
across all VMware Cloud Foundation (VCF) components. This tile gives us a quick view into the Certificates in our VCF
environment and whether they are Active or Expired.
In the following pages of this lab, we will dive deeper into each of these cards (or components) on the Diagnostics Dashboard.
HOL-2501-02-VCF-L_5.2: Getting More Out of It!

Diagnostic Findings Page - Active Findings Review

Overview pane we can see the breakdown of the high level Diagnostics Findings.
Diagnostics Findings.

In a Production
Production Environment, we may also see additional Finding Types including:
Availability, Security, Operation Diagnostics, Pre-
upgrade Check, and Product Interoperability).
3.The third section shows the distribution based on VCF component (i.e. vSphere).
In a Production
Production environment we would likely see other components including: vCenter, ESXi Hosts, NSX, VMware Aria Operations,
VMware Aria Automation, SDDC Manager, and vSAN).

Diagnostic Findings Page - Affected Objects

Affected Objects.

Production environment, we may have hundreds or thousands of objects impacted by a Finding (for instance, a Security Advisory

Diagnostics Finding Page - Recommendations

Recommendations.

=== PAGE 742 ===
The Certificates Dashboard is covered in greater detail in another lab, but here we can see a general overview of the certificates in our
environment:

as red in this chart.
2.Of the certificates being monitored, the Appliances section gives us a quick look into the various components being
monitored in the environment based upon Appliance Type.
3.The details of the status of each certificate in the environment can be exported to a CSV. The data we see in the bottom of
this chart is what can be exported to a CSV, and for a large Production Environment, we could then use that data to identify
specific areas of interest, attach to an internal ticketing system, etc.
4.Select the Filter icon
Filter icon to see the available filters of this data (not shown in the image but the data can be filtered by Status,
Appliance, Category, and Type).

Workload Provisioning Page Overview
Workload Provisioning Page Overview
On this page, we can monitor all virtual machine (VM) provisioning operations in our environment.
As an administrator, we want to determine:
•What VM operations are running in the environment?
•What is the status of these VM operations. Did they succeed or fail?
•What are the reasons for failure in cases where the workload operations fail?
The Workload Provisioning page lists the total request and failure trend over seven days which enables us to view all VM provisioning
operations in our environment, view the VM provisioning status as success or failure, and view the failure reason for each VM
provisioning operation. This page reports only the following operations: importing a vApp, creating a VM, cloning to create a new VM,
and changing a template into a VM.
1. As we can we have one virtual machine that has been provisioned in the lab environment (we did that during the prerequisite
task lesson)!

Module 16 - Understand Licensing and Certificate Management (15 minutes) Basic
Module 16 - Understand Licensing and Certificate Management (15 minutes) Basic
[861]
[861]
Introduction
Introduction
VMware Aria Operations provides a centralized plane for license management and consumption visibility. Starting with VMware Aria
Operations 8.18, you can manage and monitor the consumption of all available licenses from the registered vCenter systems in VMware
Aria Operations.
You can add new VMware Cloud Foundation Solution License keys, VMware vSphere Foundation Solution License keys, or vSAN per
TiB license keys and add them to registered vCenter systems in VMware Aria Operations.
Diagnostics fo VMware Cloud Foundation is a self-service platform that helps you analyze and troubleshoot the components of VMware
Cloud Foundation, including vCenter, ESXi, vSAN, capabilities such as vSphere vMotion, snapshots, VM provisioning, and other issues
including security advisories and certificates.
One important aspect of Diagnostics is to show relevant information about all certificates used in the VMware Cloud Foundation
deployment
In this lab, we will review how to manage your licenses in Aria Operations, see license consumption and monitor your existing
certificates in the whole VMware Cloud Foundation environment.

Introduction
Introduction
VMware Aria Operations Management Pack Builder
Aria Operations Management Pack Builder is a powerful, standalone appliance that enables you to create custom management
create custom management
packs
packs for VMware Aria Operations without any coding required. This no-code solution
no-code solution allows users to easily bring in data from external
APIs, create new resources, or extend existing VMware and third-party resources with new data, relationships, and events.
Customers should use the Aria Operations Management Pack Builder because:
1. It's easy to use
easy to use, requiring no programming skills.
2.It enables integration of data from various external sources
various external sources into VMware Aria Operations.
3.It provides a way to extend existing resources
extend existing resources with additional metrics and properties.
4.It supports creating relationships
relationships between objects.
5.It helps create comprehensive
comprehensive, custom monitoring
custom monitoring solutions for better visibility and management of IT infrastructure.
By using the Management Pack Builder, you can enhance the monitoring capabilities of VMware Aria Operations, integrate diverse data
sources, and create more comprehensive and tailored management solutions for their specific needs, all without the complexity of
traditional coding approaches.
In this lab, we will explore together the process
process of creating
creating our own Management Pack
Management Pack in a quick run-through. For this purpose, we will

VMware Aria Operations Management Pack Builder appliance, we have three options available

=== PAGE 776 ===
[890]
[890]
About the Aria Operations Connection
About the Aria Operations Connection
The VMware Aria Operations Connection
VMware Aria Operations Connection in the Management Pack Builder serves a few key purposes:
1. Extending
Extending existing objects
objects: If you want to create relationships for data objects with existing VMware Aria Operations objects,
you need to configure this connection. This allows you to extend or relate your custom objects to objects that already exist in
VMware Aria Operations.
2.Building relationships
relationships: When creating relationships between objects, you can use the connection to select existing VMware
Aria Operations resource types as parents or children for your custom objects.
3.Deploying
Deploying management packs: The connection can be used to deploy management packs directly from the Management
Pack Builder to your VMware Aria Operations instance.
4.Importing custom content
content: The connection may be used when importing custom dashboards or super metrics from an
existing VMware Aria Operations environment to include in your management pack.
It's important to note that while setting up this connection is helpful for certain features, it is not strictly necessary to get started with
creating a basic management pack. However, for more advanced features and integration with existing VMware Aria Operations objects
and data, the connection becomes very useful.
HOL-2501-02-VCF-L_5.2: Getting More Out of It!

[921]
Design Steps Overview
Design Steps Overview
Creating
Creating a new Management Pack
Management Pack using the VMware Aria Operations Management Pack Builder involves several steps
steps, some
mandatory
mandatory and some optional
optional:
Mandatory steps:
1. Define the Source
Source: Configure the target system's hostname, port, and SSL settings.
2.Set up Authentication
Authentication: Choose between Basic or Custom authentication methods.
3.Create Requests
Requests: Define API calls to retrieve data from the target system.
4.Define Objects
Objects: Create new object types or extend existing ones with metrics and properties.
5.Build
Build the Management Pack: Validate and build the final .pak file.
Optional steps:
1. Configure VMware Aria Operations Connection
Aria Operations Connection: Set up a connection to an existing Aria Operations instance for extending
objects and sample collections.
2.Create Relationships
Relationships: Define how objects relate to each other or to existing Aria Operations objects.
3.Add Events
Events: Configure events from API responses to create alerts in Aria Operations.
4.Include Custom Content
Custom Content: Import custom dashboards or super metrics to be packaged with the Management Pack.
5.Configure Advanced Settings
Advanced Settings: Adjust collection intervals and other settings for the Management Pack.
The process typically involves iterative testing and refinement before finalizing the Management Pack for deployment.
HOL-2501-02-VCF-L_5.2: Getting More Out of It!
H A N D S - O N  L A B S  M A N U A L  |  8 1 0

=== PAGE 812 ===
[923]
[923]
Steps Overview
Steps Overview
Here are short descriptions of all steps in the process of creating a new Management Pack using the Management Pack Builder. We will
learn how to use the steps in the next modules of this Lab.
1. Define the Info
Info: Set the name, description and version of the new Management Pack.
2.Define the Source
Source: Configure the target system's hostname, port, and SSL settings. Configure authentication and test
connection settings.
3.Create Requests
Requests: Define API calls to retrieve data from the target system.
4.Define Objects
Objects: Create new object types or extend existing ones with metrics and properties.
5.(optional) Create Relationships
Relationships: Establish connections between objects or link to existing Aria Operations objects.
6.(optional) Configure Events
Events: Set up events based on API responses to generate alerts in Aria Operations.
7.(optional) Add Custom Content (Customization
Customization): Import custom dashboards or super metrics to enhance the management
pack.
8.Set up Adapter Instance Configuration
Configuration: Configure the adapter instance settings for data collection.
9.Build
Build Management Pack: Generate the final .pak file for deployment in Aria Operations.
HOL-2501-02-VCF-L_5.2: Getting More Out of It!
H A N D S - O N  L A B S  M A N U A L  |  8 1 2
